The fact is that the current War Cemetery Act regulates this area flawedly and does not allow for a comprehensive procedure for detecting these graves and is, in individual parts, contrary to other laws. ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u3	For an introductory explanation of the opinion, I give the floor to the representative of the Government, the Minister for Labour, the family, social affairs and equal opportunities of Dr Anji Kopač Mrak.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u4	Honourable President of the National Assembly, Members of Parliament and Members of Parliament! The Group of Members and Members, with the first signed by Mrs Ludmilo Novak, is on 9. 4. 2015 To the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia submitted for consideration and adoption a proposal for a law on hidden war graves and burial of victims. According to the proposal, the aim of the bill is to comprehensively regulate the area of hidden war graves for all victims of war and post-war violence, thereby guaranteeing all fallen persons the right to a decent burial, to mark the grave or grave and to remember it. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia notes, in relation to the above, that the area of war graves, as well as the hidden war graves, according to the existing law, the designated graves of war victims, after war-torn persons are legally governed by the applicable war grave law. The law already governs the types of military graves, the area of war graves, the jurisdiction of the state, the scope of protection, the management and management of military graves, the register of war graves, the protection regime, the excavation or excavation process, the newly discovered war graves and financial liabilities. Under the current law, the competent ministry carries out procedures for the organisation of war graves, including hidden war graves. Thus, 600 hidden military graves, 185 probes, 60 digs, and other activities related to the protection of military graves have already been carried out. On the basis of the above, the competent ministry has already arranged a secret cemetery in the Kočev Rog area in recent years, completed the amount of remains from the karst pits, built the Teharje Memorial Park, the cemetery in Maribor and Škofja Loka. Further activities are currently under way with regard to the arrangement of the memorial park and war graves in the area of Kamnika Bistrica, activities related to the arrangement of the Barbara-rov war cemetery in the Hudi Cave and activities related to the construction of the monument for all war victims. There are also activities related to the establishment of a new information base to keep records of hidden war graves and registers of war graves. In spite of this, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia considers that the proposed law, which has been submitted, should be examined in terms of a system-wide legal aspect, whether it would be better if the objectives and purposes of the proposed law were regulated by amending the already existing law on war graves. The Government of the Republic of Slovenia understands and supports the efforts of the proposed law that all victims of war and post-war violence in the territory of the Republic of Slovenia must be granted a decent burial and thus ensure the right to a decent burial for all victims, but the question is how to achieve this in the best possible way. With this opinion, the Government of the Republic of Slovenia does not want to open up to those who promote ideological fighting and create new divisions. The purpose of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia is to re-establish Slovenia, its citizens and its inhabitants as part of an open, fair, solidarity, fair and progressive society. The final aim of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia is to join forces and find the best possible solution to all the stakeholders who are seeking to solve the decent burial of all victims of post-war, war and post-war violence. Today we take the debate primarily as an opportunity to listen to different arguments and together we find the best possible solution. Thank you.

However, the starting points must be such as to safeguard the dignity of the victims and their relatives and not to allow false interpretations of history. The law we have before us builds on the definition of the 2003 War Cemetery. The definition of a war cemetery was inappropriate at the time and inappropriate today. That is also why our Group of Members and Members of the NSI have since obstructed the adoption of the law. Let me explain why the term "war cemetery" in our opinion is controversial in at least two respects. First of all, it's not a cemetery. This is a crime scene. In these places, liquidations were carried out. It wasn't a funeral, so we can't call it a cemetery. Using the term grave means the victims already have their graves, they're already buried. Secondly, the victims were not executed either, for the execution means the taking of life on some legal basis. It was murder because there was no legal basis, no domestic, no foreign, no minimum procedures for determining guilt. The conventions prohibited this. They were exterminated on the basis of an ideological criterion. The term 'complete decent burial', which is enshrined in the law, can also mean the degradation of human dignity, as it is proposed that it merely means the marking of a newly discovered war cemetery in nature so that it can be identified. If the equality and dignity of the victims of all totalitarianism, their relatives and descendants are truly advocated, they must also be given equal standards for burial. Imagine that the worthy burial of the Srebrenica victims would be carried out only by marking the site in nature, which would then only be declared a cemetery. We cannot imagine that, so we cannot agree to such a marriage. Persistence in definitions and concepts, which have also been disputed for law-makers since 2003, can also mean reviewing historical facts, since most of the victims during the war, especially after the war, were not killed by civilians at all because of the cause of war, but because of the revolution, because of the violent takeover of power. Adherence to such definitions of so-called war victims would mean that civilian victims and victims of the revolution are identified as war victims and thus somehow justify their mass killings in terms of the war situation. It wasn't a war situation! It wasn't a war situation! Come in. The implementation of the proposed law will depend on the Commission, which will be appointed by the Government, so it will be politically appointed and will have full discretion as to whether or not the grave can be registered. So it will depend on the political decision, as it has been so far, how quickly and whether the objective of the law will be achieved. But what have these political decisions been like in the last 70 years? John Oman said that the law could only be opposed for political, ideological or prestigious reasons. Of course, it can also be said that the law can only be proposed for political, ideological or prestigious reasons. We are detained in the Slovenian Democratic Party not for political, ideological or prestigious reasons, but for principled reasons, as in 2003. The Slovenian Democratic Party does not change its opinion overnight, especially on issues that will also affect future generations and on the value of the system we will leave to our stakeholders. We will not oppose the SDS law because we welcome the reopening of the debate on the moral debt that we all have together to the victims of revolutionary violence, and we believe that the aim of the proposaler is well-intentioned. However, since the law in the form before us does not solve the underlying issues previously raised and remains within the same substantive framework as the 2003 law, there is no reason for us to act this time unintentionally and irresponsibly. Especially in the light of the fact that the law is also restricted by the representatives of those to whom the law is primarily intended. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u8	Dear President, distinguished Minister, distinguished colleagues and colleagues! Allow me to give you a brief overview of the position of the Desus Group on the draft law on hidden military graves and the burial of victims. Since the proposals, colleagues in Nova Slovenija presented the first draft of the law on a decent burial, we have been detained in the Desus Group and I must say that our position, despite the fact that the proposal has been corrected and changed the name of the law, has not changed significantly. Let me explain. Our main concerns remain. First time. The current War Cemetery Act regulates all that is proposed in the new law, so we are concerned about the duplication of legislation and the possible confusion that the new law can cause, as certain duties and solutions are, as is said, disguised. Another time. The opinion of the RS Government is also very reserved. In addition, it is a legally poor proposal for a law, since at least 18 articles of the proposed regulation should be amended if the law came into another reading. These are all the articles. Then what's the point of all this? At the same time, experience teaches us that the law, which is largely an amendment, does not provide a good road map for its effective implementation in practice as well. Third time. The War Cemeteries Act, which was adopted in 2003, is currently being amended in 2009. We are confident that with any appropriate amendments we can improve the existing law that defines the subject of war graves. More. We are also convinced that the law in force on war graves actually regulates everything that is the basis of the new law, but that it must be implemented. A similar opinion is shared by a very broad civil and social public. The fact is that the War Cemetery Act is being implemented slowly and imperfectly. It is the same with the Law on the Protection of Cultural Heritage, even in the work that speaks of war graves as an archaeological and memory heritage, for which there is no serious or valid excuse. Fourth time. In view of all of this, there is no need, but really no need to adopt such rules, which result in additional financial obligations, which in the assessment of the financial consequences of the applicant amount to between EUR 500 000 and EUR 700 000 per annum. In order not to be misunderstood here, financial means, one or the other, must by no means be and cannot be an excuse for a silent attitude toward all the dead. I repeat, the fifth attitude towards all the dead, including their worthy burial, is one of the key objectives of the government, which I am sure will also be realized. In doing so, I would also like to express my joy at the fact that it is practical before the construction of the monument to the victims of all wars, for which many of us have been working a long time ago. Colleagues and colleagues! In doing so, the Government and the competent institutions shall call on them to adopt a concrete and binding operational plan for the management of war graves in order to provide sufficient financial resources in the budget of the Republic of Slovenia to properly organise and, where appropriate, strengthen the service for war graves, which will have adequate and sufficient powers to do its work. It is necessary to establish clear boundaries and competences between the Ministry of War Cemetery Service and the Commission of the Government of the Republic of Slovenia to address the issues of hidden graves. It is necessary to check how the municipalities carry out the tasks relating to war graves and the protection of monuments and memorials and to take appropriate measures. It must be ensured that the Institute for Recent History will be able to complete the register and analysis of Slovenian deaths in World War II. It is necessary to commit all competent authorities and services to carry out their tasks in the field of war graves with priority, efficiency and full responsibility and to inform the National Assembly accordingly and continuously. Dear colleagues, dear colleagues! I sincerely hope that at this point it is not a matter of gathering political points at the expense of sad human destiny and personal tragedies. There is no need to adopt a new law, a concrete action plan, possibly amendments, to properly organise work, to remove all administrative obstacles and procedures that have repeatedly plagued work in this area, and, above all, to bow down and express clearly the will to achieve common objectives. Dear colleagues, dear colleagues! In the Desus Group, we will continue to strive to achieve a universally respectful and unconditional, I repeat a unconditional, pitiful attitude towards all the dead, including the establishment of a central monument for the victims of all wars. In doing so, we will support solutions that will not cause separation but that will connect us. ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u10	Dear Mr President, colleagues and colleagues! Social Democrats The draft law on hidden war graves and burial of victims, submitted by a group of Members and Members, with the first signed Ludmila Novak, submitted to the National Assembly of the Republic of Slovenia, will not be supported. We are convinced that the applicable War Cemetery Act at systemic level deals with all the fundamental issues raised by the applicants as subject of this law. Nor do social democrats accept the thesis and frequent hints that the current War Cemetery Act is not being implemented and that, in particular, in the area of hidden war graves, nothing has been done since the law was enacted that is far from the truth. If we want to speak objectively and reliably about this proposal for a law before us today, it is therefore essential that we shed some light on the events of past mandates in the handling and adoption of so-called war laws, to which this bill certainly also belongs. The theme of war laws is to deal with various issues of half-lived history concerning the time of World War II and the time after the end of World War II. Whether we want it or not, the fact remains that there are always, in particular, those issues that we perceive in different ways after the 1970s, and we have different feelings for them, because we take them differently and value them. And from here, unfortunately, we are only one step away from this, which is often referred to as an ideological struggle and a new division among people, which I believe no one wants between us. This is highlighted by the Social Democrats because, from the experience of the previous mandates of the National Assembly, we know that, as a rule, the debate on the open issues of past history has been treated differently than we do today. It was coordinated talks with representatives of all parliamentary political parties, as well as representatives of veteran and patriotic organisations, which sought to reach as wide a consensus as possible on a number of sensitive issues, which would push the way towards common objectives that we understand as mutual respect, reconciliation, a five-way relationship with the dead and reconciliation. We then understood, as we understand today, that these issues cannot and must not be in the field of political prestige and dominance of one party or parliamentary group, if we are truly bona fide and we want to resolve these issues in a way that will not leave anyone with bitter taste and will dispel everyone's minimum doubts as to the correctness and fairness of common decisions. Some ten years ago, when the competent Minister Drobnic of the political party from which the billers come from today prepared drafts of war laws, awareness prevailed among the political parties of today that it did not matter how to deal with such issues. We social democrats have always worked hard to do this. Although considerable time and effort have been put into discussing and coordinating legal solutions, the 2004-2008 mandate has failed to achieve sufficient harmonisation and the legislative process on war laws has not continued. However, the effort and search for solutions to these laws continued in the mandate when we led the government coalition by the Social Democrats and in 2009 a very important amendment to the War Cemetery Act was adopted, which was already prepared by Minister Drobnič in the basic solutions. The law was coordinated in the widest possible political round, as it was also supported by the opposition Slovenian People's Party in addition to the four left-wing government parties. According to our belief, the novel of the War Cemetery Act has brought important solutions that we do not yet know today or that we do not want to properly evaluate, namely, the novel predicted that in Ljubljana a monument will be erected to all victims of war and to wars related to the victims in Slovenia with the dedicating inscription of Oton Župančič, the Homeland is one, given to all of us, and one life and one death. She predicted that for the remains of persons who would not have a separate cemetery or grave, the central tomb would be placed in the Teharje Memorial Park area. It also envisioned different texts of the consecrated nature at the graves, where the graves of civilian victims of war and after the war of the dead were written: Victims of war and revolutionary violence, Republic of Slovenia. It is envisaged that the family may be allowed to transfer the remains of the deceased to another grave at the same or another cemetery. It provided for more precise provisions on the recording of military graves and clarified the legal basis for the protection of the military graves of the Republic of Slovenia abroad, which are regulated by interstate agreements or arrangements. It is therefore not possible to speak honestly today about the fact that nothing has been done in the area of the editing and detection of hidden war graves. In its opinion, the government has made very clear what activities it has already carried out in the past concerning the hidden war sites. Thus, 600 hidden military graves, 185 probes, 60 burials and excavations were carried out. In recent years, the government has arranged a hidden war cemetery in the Kočevský Rog area, the amount of remains from the karst plains was built, the Teharje Memorial Park, a built skeleton in Maribor and Škofja Loka. Other editing activities are also taking place at various sites of war graves and activities to build a monument to all victims of war in Ljubljana. Finally, activities are under way to establish a new information base to keep records of hidden war graves and registers of war graves. It is true that the scope of the activity in this area depends primarily on the ability to finance or on the annual amount of budgetary resources allocated to the regulation of war graves, particularly during public financial savings in all areas. There is probably no way to deny that more could be done in this area in recent years. However, this fact does not give no one the right to deceive by arguing that the State has done nothing in this area. As it is said, the Social Democrats do not share the view that a special law must be adopted to better and better regulate hidden war graves. The applicants themselves in the introduction of the legislative proposal state that this area is governed by the War Cemetery Act, which is being implemented too slowly after them, stressing that the implementation of the law depends largely on the political will of each government to resolve this area, including the provision of funds for this purpose. Therefore, the applicants themselves find that, in their view, in the current legislation, there is not so much a problem in the regulation itself as they consider a problem in the absence of political will and budgetary money. So why a special marriage? The applicants further point out that, in the case of hidden graves, the entry of such graves into the register is impervious, but this cannot be a valid reason for a specific law. We have not received a convincing answer to this question to this day, so we do not see any serious argument as to why a specific special law, full of such provisions, would be adopted as a specific type of war grave, which by their very nature fall within the law and not into law. We are confident that by adopting such a law, we would introduce too much legal confusion, and that one category of graves would be excluded from the system regime, as we know today in the War Cemetery Act. Social Democrats also discussed this proposal for a law on hidden war graves and the burial of victims with representatives of the Union of National Liberation Fighters, who in the past have always actively cooperated in the search for common solutions when so-called war laws were on the agenda. At this point, we share the view that this law is neither appropriate nor necessary in its content, since we have this area quite well regulated in the War Cemetery Act. We also share the view that in this area something should be improved in particular in the more effective implementation of the applicable law. Social Democrats are not opposed to a decent burial and reconciliation, but this is not linked to the question of whether or not we support this bill. Our reservation stems from the belief that, with regard to the amendment of the existing law, there has not been a broader discussion of all stakeholders, and thus we do not have the views of all stakeholders, nor do we have convincing substantive explanations from the proposer as to what is preventing or impeding the implementation of the law, if we are talking only about its content and if we leave the country with the capacity to provide higher budgetary resources. On the basis of all this, we will therefore vote against the conclusion that the proposal for a law on hidden war graves and burial of victims is suitable for further consideration. Thank you very much.

However, because of all the well-known circumstances, disagreements and conflicts after Slovenia's independence, the strict ignorance of the single party regime towards this issue, and the atmosphere that was then created that we did not even mention unilateral interpretations of history, unfortunately so far we were not able to fully and consistently regulate it. Therefore, a correction of the legal regime is necessary, in our view, in one way or another. In recent months, this has been quite uniquely discussed by most political and social actors. Prime Minister Cerar also spoke yesterday in response to the question before me about such a context. In short, we hope that there is a consensus on the need for a comprehensive solution to this sensitive issue, and that we should not be reopened to conflict areas that would continue to separate us from each other in the search for a legal upgrade. Finally, a step forward in the right direction should be taken in concrete terms, which may also be to some extent a support for this draft law in the first reading. However, even if there is no support, the regulation of this important task must not be stopped. Although we have different views on some concrete solutions to the proposed law on our part, this material can serve as a starting point for further consideration and completion of the text. We have foreseen that, according to the previous positions, I can no longer argue that the greatest debate and careful consideration will be required in the search for the correct form of operation and the composition of the commission to deal with cases relating to hidden graves, in the search for the most prudent form of work and in the preparation of the register of all hidden and other graves and in relation to financial matters. Nevertheless, it is likely that it will be necessary to hold on to a number of consensus, whether it is so or otherwise, but it must be our shared responsibility that possible differences do not stop the whole process again. We believe that some solutions can also take good practice from other countries. Nobody's actually talked about this today, even though we've got something like that. ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u52	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Respectable law-makers! I think you've now seen a picture of what that real core of the problem is. And as I understand it, that was exactly the purpose of your law to avoid it and to achieve something that was inherent to human civilization or should be inherent. The proposal for the Law on Hidden War Cemeteries, of course, agrees in the Modern Centre Party when we talk about the declared intentions highlighted by the applicant. This is to accelerate the resolution of the issue of hidden graves in such a way that they will be regulated and transformed into conventional regulated war graves. I think that there is rarely anyone in this Chamber, but also outside it, who would object to the issue of the inappropriate last resting place of a group of people or individuals being settled in a dignified and civilized manner; of course, within the limits of the possibility. For in history, the classic last resting place could never be provided to all people. Wars of storms and horrors have often broken the minimum standards that still make a man. Such sad cases are not limited to World War II; they have occurred before, and unfortunately, because of the effects of time that pushes them all into oblivion, we have pushed them out of our collective memory. For example, I remember that in the village around Ljubljana, where I lived, I walked almost every day past the old farm house, which was abandoned at the time, today is a multi-housing facility. It was a touching memory board that the master of this house built. Because both sons fell in World War I, and he did not know where they were lying, he built a monument for them in the wall of the house. It said that they fought bravely in Galicia for their homeland, then Austria. He wrote something else: The memory of you, dear sons, will live as long as it is the foundation of this house. The foundation of this house no longer stands. But it's just as an illustration that with the right to a decent grave, it's as sad as the right to work for young people. In principle, it is supported and, unfortunately, it is never possible to achieve this for everyone and for all. Today, too, the goals outlined in the bill are the right to a grave or a decent burial, something that is in line with reality. This reality consists not only of political will but also of the human resources and financial capacities that are actually available today and here, that is, in today's situation in which our country and society are. However, we attach the purpose of the law, particularly because the proposaler points out that the law does not intend to open ideological debates. And that's exactly the limit that we're going to keep in the SMC Group. The purpose of the law is acceptable to us until it turns out that someone would like to use it for ideological debates and a revision of the outcome of World War II. We support the aims of the law to try to change the universally recognized civilisational conclusions, who was on the right side of World War II and who was not. If we agree with the stated objectives of the law, we have serious concerns about the solutions that the proposal for a law brings. That's what my friend Zorčič was talking about. These concerns are considerable and I do not intend to mention everyone at this point, but – I would like to know the most which body the applicant had as an example when he wrote about what powers the government commission should have for research into hidden graves. These are very large in the bill. The Commission itself lays down the conditions of action, powers and tasks; however, it is unclear to whom it is responsible for its work. Not only does the Commission itself adopt a work plan, which is then approved by the government, but it also adopts the rules on which it works. She assigns her own fees, and for administrative matters, she thinks of herself as secretary-general of the Directorate-General of the competent ministry; and if she fails to obey the members of the commission properly enough, they can propose to the Minister his replacement. This was explicitly stated by the applicant in the draft law. In short, the proposer foresees that the Commission will have very large powers, which, under the Law on State Administration, belong to the Ministry or State Administration. This is something that may be contrary to the principle of legal protection, since, according to the proposal, a commission that is not a national authority can, for example, take certain measures to restrict the use of their property by the owner of the land where the remains are to be located. But it's not clear who the owners can complain to. Let me point out something that I personally have been doing for 25 years in the field of culture, the most concerned and which I strongly oppose. This is a derogation from the Law on the Protection of Cultural Heritage. It is a provision in Article 9 of the law that the provisions of the Law on the Protection of Cultural Heritage do not apply when it comes to locations where the remains are located. I believe that this is a very harmful provision which can cause irreparable consequences to cultural heritage. By the way, the Cultural Heritage Protection Act was drafted by the government of Janez Janša and was adopted in early 2008; in the mandate of the SDS with its coalition, it had a majority in Parliament. So it's probably not some swindler of dark forces who would object to the funeral of the victims of the extrajudicial beatings. Article 3 of the Law on the Protection of Cultural Heritage, which is intended to be repealed by the proposal for a law, reads: "Archaeological remains are also matters related to graves established on the basis of the rules on war graves and by war, together with archaeological and natural contexts that have been underground or underwater for at least 50 years. Professionally identified and registered archaeological remains become an inheritance." The locations where the remains are located also contain other material that provides valuable information about the event itself, battle, execution, and similar. It is important that the archaeological method is carefully investigated to preserve the integrity of the context of the site. Also in order to ensure accuracy of information, what happened and how it actually happened, what happened – who were the dead, who were the executioners, who were the participants in the battle or so. The latter is indispensable to our past behavior. The very location of the find is, if its importance is determined by an archaeological profession, it can be declared a cultural monument or entered into a register of cultural heritage. This is under the responsibility of the Institute for the Protection of the Cultural Heritage of Slovenia and cannot be decided by the Commission for the Study of Hidden Cemeteries. These are, therefore, some of the shortcomings that I believe should be addressed in the next procedure in the proposal for a law if it is to be considered further. Thank you.

The family had a nice farm, they even had a mill. As a three - month - old child, he was taken along with only a few years - old brothers and sisters to a German camp. His father or my grandfather was taken to the National Socialist concentration camp Dachau. My grandmother was taken as a mother by four children to the Celje Old Writer and shot. The real reasons are unknown. As a mother of a baby — a two - month - old baby — and three entirely small children were to act against the invader. Upon returning home, the household where they had lived before was torn apart, much destroyed and estranged, you will not believe where they had seen their own things. But it was not possible to compare this with the fact that the children and the husband were without a mother and a wife. The postwar authorities then took away the cow, which was a source of survival for these young children. They sat on the hill when they watched the authorities drive or take what meant food to them. But somehow they got through. They never knew where the mother’s grave was, but her name was found on monuments in the home congregation and also in Gradac. Her name is on the family grave today. It's where we light candles all the time. After that, she was named after a village elementary school. My father never knew his mother. As a result, I didn't know my grandmother. However, in 2012 they were also allocated to approximately the same amount of finance as the year or two earlier and as the year, two or three later. As regards the cases carried out in the years 2012 to April 2015, this includes the regular maintenance of 120 war graves in the whole of Slovenia; the maintenance and management of the Dobrava cemetery in Maribor; the amount of post-mortem remains from 45 karst caves and the storage in the Dobrava cemetery in Maribor; the arrangements for the covert Barbara trench war cemetery, this part relating to the decisions of the mining inspection; the cooperation with the Ministry of Defence in the discovery of the military cemetery in the area of the Cerklje military airport near Krka and the preservation of the skeletons from this area in the Dobrava cemetery in Maribor; the execution of the competition and preparation of the project documentation for the construction of a monument to all victims of war; the cooperation in the organisation of the memorial park and the hidden war plots in the area of Kamniška Bistrica; the redemption of the land in the area of the hidden war cemetery in Maribor; the construction and the preparation of the project documentation for the construction of a monument to all victims of the wares; and the inter-refination of the period of the burial of the war. Ordering and rehabilitation of the Kozarje War Cemetery; rehabilitation and arrangement of the World War I Cemetery in Trenta; intervention arrangements of the 1st World War Cemetery in Ukanac and Rebro; intervention arrangements of the War Cemetery and the Russian Chapel in Vršič; arrangements of the War Cemetery and memorial park for the Francolovic victims; and co-financing of the Sočje project — Ways of Peace. It has already been said in the opening that 600 such alleged graves have been entered in the records of the hidden graves; that the probes were carried out in 185 cases, that 99 of these graves have been confirmed as graves, that there are actually remains in there; and that 86 such graves have not been confirmed. As for the editing of Barbara Rowe — the Barbara Reef Cemetery, Huda Jama, was discovered in March 2009. And that's when the construction of the skeleton began, which was completed by the end of 2009. This skeleton provides a suitable, five - foot - long burial or storage. The cemetery was registered in the War Cemetery Register in November 2012. The Ministry set up a memorial board at the mine entrance and arranged a room for candles and flowers. The Ministry has concluded an agreement with the owner of the mine Pivovarno Laško on the management of mine premises and, on the basis of this agreement, the owner allows access to the war cemetery. The owner is obliged to take care of the safety conditions and also of the safety of visitors. On the basis of the status acquired for the war grave, the regime laid down in Article 30 of the War Cemetery Act entered into force for the areas of the war grave. Last year's activities, according to 2014 and 2015, were carried out mainly in the direction of the rehabilitation of the mine shaft on the basis of a mining inspection decision. These works were completed on 11 March this year and some EUR 248,000 was spent on these works. From the beginning of the discovery of the Barbara trench cemetery until the end of 2011, about a million euros were allocated to the site. The costs of identification and investigation were borne by the Ministry of Justice and the Ministry of the Interior and should amount to about half a million euros. This year, the Ministry consulted experts who believe that postmortem remains are suitable for permanent storage after the decommissioning process, so we conduct procedures to ensure permanent and more appropriate preservation of postmortem remains or so-called coffin storage. Can I call it that? Could more be done, or could individual works, detections, probes take place more quickly? You could, of course you could. Or in certain circumstances, it was impossible to say that we were not working in accordance with what we were committed to and what we were able to do, based on the procedures that needed to be followed. It was said that reconciliation is really a process; that reconciliation cannot be the result of one act. We've been talking about this process for at least the last 25 years, in our history. Are we actually going against this thing? Whether we agree on what this reconciliation should mean, what it should be and what it should accomplish is another question. Because of the fact that there is some inner reconciliation, the consensus of generations, the peace of living, and the capacity to behave to all victims must be precisely that — consensus. And while there is no consensus, it is difficult to say that we are taking steps in the same direction. We can take steps, but these steps can be the opposite. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u114	Mr President, thank you for the floor. Ladies and gentlemen of the House! The proposal for a law on tax clearance of accounts is an integral part of measures to combat the grey economy. This measure will introduce so-called tax receipts in the form of an invoice validation procedure with the Financial Administration. The clearance procedure will be mandatory for all persons who issue accounts in cash transactions. The basic objective of this process of compulsory clearance of accounts is to establish a system that will allow for transparent and reliable recording of the cash traffic of taxable persons, thereby reducing the scope of the grey economy and preventing tax evasion and fraud in the field of value added tax, corporate income taxes, operating income tax, income tax and other benefits, as well as the proper calculation of social security contributions and the control of the legality of employment or work. According to the proposal of the law, cashiers will be linked online to the central information system of the Financial Administration, which will confirm and store account details in the real-time issuance process. The procedure for tax clearance of accounts, together with the provisions in force under Articles 31a and 38 of the Tax Procedure Act, will create a system that will allow traceability and effectiveness of controls over invoices issued and contribute to the limitation of the grey economy. The bill also provides for a transitional period within which the debtors will be allowed to gradually switch to the system of clearance of accounts. The transitional period will take two years, up to 31. 12. 2017, and at that time, the debtors will be able to decide on their own whether to use an electronic device with established electronic connection to the Financial Administration or accounts from the associated account book, which will be required subsequently, within ten working days, to confirm with the Financial Administration. The optional implementation of the clearance procedure shall be determined by 1. 12. 2015, which facilitates the preparation of the debtors for the mandatory implementation of the clearance procedure provided for from 2 January 2016. From the introduction of mandatory clearance of accounts during the year of the introduction of this measure, we expect an increase in tax revenues of between €50 million and €100 million. This is an additional impact to be attributed to the financial impact of the measures already introduced to combat the grey economy. The proposed law is an important element of the fight against the grey economy, and it does not, in any way, provide or provide an answer to all the questions we face in relation to the fight against the grey economy, so it will have to be strengthened or complemented with complementary activities, on the one hand, with an adequate analysis of the risk of the data to be obtained by the Financial Administration in the validation process, as well as the appropriate use of available human resources on the Financial Administration. With all due regard to the above, I propose that you support the proposal for a law and that the National Assembly decide that the law is appropriate for further consideration. Thank you very much.

We all know that the social costs of resolving the crisis have been very high in recent years, which has had a particular impact on the increase in public financial expenditure mainly due to the resolution of the succeeding financial sector. As a result of this, the cuts in other items on the expenditure side of the budget have been said to be savings, making the social picture in Slovenia more and more acute today. That is why, in the United Left, we believe that it is high time for us to start solving the social crisis, or now the public finances crisis, on the other hand, by increasing the budget revenue, so that we can mitigate the pressures on the budget spending side. The tax remittances – i.e. the proposal before us – partially solve this problem because they are expanding the taxpayers' base by limiting the grey economy and will contribute positively to the resolution of the crisis in this way, so the United Left has the support of this proposal in principle. Although the proposal for the introduction of the tax registers is not new, it has never been successful due to the various pressures of the stakeholders so far. Various employers' associations and some media have tried to give the public the impression that the tax is just a cost and will not have an effect. This is not true, since facts and experiences from other countries show that tax collectors actually contribute to a more equal treatment of taxpayers, it is said that they reduce the possibility of tax evasion and that they also increase tax revenues from this title. Tax registers have proved to be a good solution in some countries, and there have been quite a few positive experiences with us since the introduction of so-called virtual tax registers. However, we often hear an argument that even after the introduction of the tax box, those entrepreneurs who cheat in such a way that they do not issue the invoices will still not issue the invoices and therefore will still not pay the taxes. That is true, but it is important to note that many cheats, for example, by issuing an account and then not recording it in business books or by deleting it afterwards. Well, it seems outside that he's issued a bill to do business legally, but he doesn't take the tax away from that income later. This is not only about VAT, but also ultimately about the income of independent entrepreneurs or the corporate income tax when it comes to larger businesses. Tax receipts seem useful to us because they limit the possibility of such abuses. Although we have so far had virtual cash registers, where the deletion or change of accounts has been prohibited, it has not been technically impossible. But in the first step, it is said by introducing virtual cash registers, according to the applicant, the rate of payment of taxes has increased, so the introduction of fair tax boxes in this sense is a step forward and we support it in the United Left, as we expect positive effects. Another reason why we support the introduction of tax registers is to facilitate their control. As long as we have only virtual tax receipts, i.e. a legal ban on erasure of accounts, the infringement inspectors can only detect by field control. The proposal that we have before us, however, provides for an online system of tax receipts, is that the tax authority, on the other hand, obtains traffic data for each issued cash account. The inspectors may exercise control over this system without having to visit the debtor himself. In our opinion, the online data recording system brings another novelty that we support. When FURS has this data at its disposal, it will be able to identify, on the basis of statistics, which debtors are most likely to tax evasion or to violate labour legislation. Once the FURS has all this information on the table, it will be able to carry out targeted controls. This will make it easier for FURS to prosecute both tax evasion and infringements of labour rights on the other hand. In fact, companies that only pay with cash, which only deal in cash, have the greatest potential for black employment. In recent times, there has been a growing practice of hiring a company or a self-employed worker, but only for reduced working hours and in fact working full-time or even more. Only part-time work is taking place because half of the money, half of the wages are paid and if he loses his job, he is therefore entitled only to a minimum unemployment allowance. Of course, workers do not often hope to resist such evasion and violation of labour rights in fear of jobs, and, on the other hand, they are unjustifiably poor in terms of both the health and the pension and the state budget, because of half the wages paid by the worker in black, no contributions and income are paid. In our opinion, with the proposed tax registers, FURS would be able to link the data from the account records to the employment records and by comparing these two types of data, it detects violations of labour law and fundamental labour rights. In the public debate, the tax boxes have been ignored by this law to prosecute infringements of labour rights, but in the United Left we consider that this is one of the main reasons why the introduction of these registers makes sense. In recent months, FURS has finally begun to prosecute employers who are forcing their employees to become independent entrepreneurs instead of hiring them. With the introduction of tax registers, without any budgetary costs in this control, we could take a huge step forward not only against tax dumping but also against social dumping. And the third time. There were a lot of fears in the public about the costs that the debtors would have for the introduction of the new system. Here it must be said that the exemptions under the Tax Procedure Act are fully maintained, both for farmers and for entrepreneurs with few cash items, even in the future, there will be no need for tax receipts. However, it will not be necessary to buy new cash registers or new computer programs for those debtors who are already obliged by the current rules to keep business books and have cash registers. Small pledges will have a free FURS programme at their disposal, and already existing debtors will need only a system upgrade, so this problem does not seem relevant to us. Finally, I would stop at the obligations imposed by the law on consumers. The consumer union here has pointed out high fines, problematic fines if the customer does not save an account for goods or services. This argument is partly agreed with us, because it makes no sense to penalise someone with 40 euros of fines if they reject, say, an invoice for 10 euros, so it would make sense to think here about a threshold, a lower limit in such criminal provisions and also a pardon for the fines themselves, because they are actually too high. On the other hand, we are not in favour of the complete deletion of the criminal provisions in this Article, since buyers are not only natural persons, not only final consumers, but often businesses, legal entities that can participate on purpose, help other companies in tax evasion in order to buy cheaper goods. That's why it makes sense to us to some extent. In the United Left, we support the law with these comments, but we note that regulation also bears a great responsibility. The FURS should now be able to ensure the easy and smooth and free use of these applications. On the other hand, it is a political responsibility in government. In fact, spreading the tax base is one step towards tax fairness and another step towards tax fairness is intensifying tax progress so that the country collects more tax mainly for people with higher incomes and more assets. Only when the country starts collecting taxes more effectively than the most wealthy will we be able to start thinking about reducing VAT and then eventually less reasons for doing business without accounts. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u124	President, Secretary of State, colleagues and colleagues! Since the introduction of so-called tax quasi-cassettes, i.e. from 1 July 2013, Slovenia has been discussing whether these registers are sufficient or should we, in the case of neighbouring Croatia, also introduce the so-called real tax registers. To refresh my memory, I would like to remind you of the spectacular actions of Croatian authorities that closed the guesthouses in the middle of the tourist season in 2013. Many Slovenian tourists were then approving the Croatian tax authorities' film actions and we were all discussing their success. And if I stay a little longer with our neighbours and remember how the introduction of the tax registers took place with them; the Fiscalisation Act was adopted in Croatia in November 2012 and introduced in three phases. The first phase envisaged the introduction of cash registers for large and medium-sized enterprises and connected around 22,000 debtors. The second phase brought together other debtors, such as merchants, athletes, lawyers, notaries, doctors, and connected around 57,000. The third stage was an obligation for all debtors. With 1. Seven. In Croatia, the use of tax warehouses has become compulsory for all taxpayers. In the middle of the tourist season, we were able to observe on one side of the already mentioned spectacular action by Croatian tax authorities, and on the other hand we were able to read in the daily newspaper about the unexpected good results of the introduction of tax registers. Thus, the debtors reported on average up to 40% more traffic than the year before. These are certainly data worthy of additional attention, media and political attention. In about the same time frame, we have also tried to infect the grey economy with different receptions. The amendment adopted in December 2012 on the Tax Procedure Act entered into force on 1 July 2013. The essential difference between Croatia and our regulation is that the tax administration is immediately informed after Croatian traffic arrangements and the situation should be checked at the treasury location or wait for the debtor to report. Remember, the amendment to the Tax Procedure Act provided that a taxable person should not have or use computer programs or electronic devices that enable the deletion, repair or any modification of the record in the device. If I simplify, traffic from such devices can no longer be deleted or modified. And also the Slovenian version of the tax box significantly increased the inflow of tax duties in the first year of introduction. Data from 2014 show that, due to the use of registers, the country collected 24 % more VAT per month in certain activities. Data for October 2014 show, for example, that 39.9 % more VAT was declared in the service, 40 % more in the production of bread, pastry and pastries, and 145 % more in the sale of textiles on the market. Both variants, Croatia and Slovenia, thus pursue the same objective, namely to reduce the scope of the grey economy by introducing a system that will allow transparent and reliable recording of cash transactions. It follows from the proposal for a law on tax clearance of accounts that we have before us today that the law will provide for the obligation to carry out the procedures for the validation of accounts in cash for tax purposes, to determine who will be liable to carry out the clearance of accounts procedure and the clearance of accounts procedure itself, to determine the content of the account for the purposes of carrying out the clearance of accounts procedure, to determine the technical requirements, to control the implementation of the law and to us a very controversial obligation to take over the invoice issued in cash transactions for the buyer. We do not, in principle, object to the proposal for a law that we are considering today in the context of the first reading, in the Group of Members of the Alliance. While there are some provisions in the text itself that we expect to be complemented, corrected or excluded from the text in future. There are also some open questions that we expect to receive appropriate answers. However, it is totally unacceptable for us to accept the third paragraph of Article 12 and Article 17 of the draft law, which lays down sanctions in cases where the consumer cannot submit an invoice for this to the authorised person. The tax authority is the one responsible for overseeing the work and reporting of taxpayers and not the consumer! In cases where the taxpayer's tax authority finds irregularities in the debtor's case, it may or must punish them – the debtors and not the consumers. The consumer's punishment in this case is understood as a transfer of responsibility for the competence to control the work of the debtors and especially when a fine of up to €400 is at risk for the consumer. We believe that the questions raised by the Chamber of Commerce of Slovenia are such that further consideration is needed. Finally, the Croatian law also removed some activities by taxi. Once again, I would like to stress that the tax warehouses are intended to curb the scope of the grey economy that arises when paid in cash. I point out – what I am saying now – because the law also does not provide for a different treatment of those who do not deal with cash. An appropriate supplement will certainly be necessary, according to our assessment. We agree that the exception should not, in principle, be, or should not be, at all, in the so-called ‘paushalists’, where the precise coverage of the transport data created by the debtor is of the utmost importance. As has already been said and followed by my presentation, the Alliance's Group will support the law at first reading and our support will depend on whether our comments will be duly taken into account. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u126	Dear representatives of the Ministry of Finance, honourable Members, honourable Members! In line with the pre-election SMC programme, which later became a major part of the coalition contract, we are now finally starting with the first reading of the draft law on tax clearance of accounts, which is often referred to in the public as a tax box law. The grey economy is estimated by the Statistical Office of the Republic of Slovenia to exceed 8% of GDP, which is translated into money at around EUR 3 billion. That the problem is pressing and that it must be addressed primarily by changes in tax legislation, we are told that the main part is tax evasion. In the SMC, we are aware that everything cannot be moved from a grey field, but we believe that with the proposed law and complementary measures in this area we can do much more, as part of the reduction in the volume of the grey economy will also be reflected in the higher revenues of the state budget and thus indirectly shed some of the most pressing issues of this time: why there is a shortage of money for social transfers, for pensions, for health care, why the wages of some of our country's basic builders are poor, why there is no level of service or service provided by our country higher. With the tax confirmation of accounts, we will cover part of the grey economy that is particularly growing in cash transactions, undetected cash traffic with the final consumer is expanding upwards through the supply chain or production chain, and puts employees in a difficult position who can sometimes receive even the bulk of income in cash. It is therefore a matter of eliminating the negative multiplier effect on the economy due to the distortion of the competitive position of companies on the market and, as a result, the uneven distribution of tax burdens among the debtors, the additional pressure it exerts on the exaggeration of workers and work and on employment in black, the long-term questionable position of employees who will once retire and will have difficulty living their fall due to low pension bases, and the significant effects on public finances, which also impose disproportionate burdens on future generations today due to such shortfalls. The removal of negative effects is at the same time a prerequisite for facilitating restructuring or a deliberate reduction of the burden on labour income. The urgency of changes in cash management also shows the disproportionate burden on tax inspectors on cash controls. Over the past years, more than half of them have been involved in the inspection of this area, with the introduction of tax confirmation of SMC accounts, we anticipate that at least the following positive effects will occur in the area of tax control, control and investigation. In particular, the transfer of much of the latency tasks now carried out by inspectors to the information system, which will be able to detect and expose advanced algorithms and potential tax evasion, thus allowing inspectors to focus only on high-risk entities. By means of targeted controls, the efficiency of inspectors and the reduced administrative burden on debtors acting in an exemplary manner will thus make it easier to counter a downward trend in the number of tax inspectors. We anticipate that this will also lead to a shift of more than 100 tax inspectors to more complex cases, and to a more efficient and easier exercise of the tasks entrusted to tax controllers, and not least to a more effective combination and coordination of labour with market and labour inspectors. In the SMC, I am aware that the effectiveness of the proposed system can strongly trigger possible exceptions and therefore, except for solid reasons, we are not inclined to do so. As such, we understand, in particular, the potential mechanical equipment failures, the communication network, the power outages and the lack of access to the Internet. The proposal for a solution for such cases is already foreseen. During the public debate, it was apparent that no exception was envisaged for the smaller debtors, which is why two solutions are envisaged, which are likely to have to be further developed, but are considered a good extension to the upcoming tax system. During the transitional period, the proposal allows for the possibility to choose between the real-time tax clearance or the use of a linked account book with subsequent confirmation, the first option being offered in the form of an online account release application. The application will be offered free of charge by the Financial Administration of the Republic of Slovenia, which we welcome in the SMC, as we estimate that a large majority of NGOs and other small individuals have at least one member or co-worker with a smartphone or other device that simultaneously has access to the world's Internet. We are also aware of the additional administrative burden to be borne by companies. For this reason, the measure was announced already a good year ago in the pre-election programme of the SMC, then again in the coalition contract last autumn, in the action plan for the introduction of the tax registers at the beginning of this year, and we now rightly expect this law to be enforced 6 months before the application of the statutory provisions becomes mandatory. In contrast to the administrative burdens that we are also advocating and preparing in the SMC and will be announced in a short time, we believe that a reasonable increase in the administrative burden should be announced substantially earlier in order for the debtors to properly prepare themselves, specific administrative burdens are currently being prepared to a greater extent and, in our opinion, a slightly shorter period before the imposition of administrative burdens on the debtors should not cause problems such as the possible introduction of tax goods just prior to the present. Slovenia has no special features in the introduction of the tax registers, many countries already know them, including 15 Member States of the European Union, and it is expected that we will soon join some others. A key difference is that Slovenia introduces a more modern system, since countries that have established systems years ago, for example in the 1990s, have not yet made such progress in information technologies. The solution provided for by the bill, i.e. through automatic electronic clearance of accounts at the Financial Administration, has only become possible with an exceptional increase in data transmission capacity over the Internet, the progress of electronic devices, software and the possibility of effective and secure electronic communication and signing. This was an opportunity for a more cost-effective and administratively less demanding system for the state and the debtors, which will deliver significantly better results. The initial concerns about the high costs associated with the purchase of specific certified software and hardware are thus eliminated. An open approach, which only provides for a definition of the way in which data are exchanged with the Financial Administration, will avoid restrictions on any of the special and generally more expensive solutions on the market. The market for the development of business support software will therefore be open to all, including marriage companies and young information technology experts, who will be able to find their own business path in writing innovative and user-friendly computer programs and smart phone applications, which will be used in future to issue cash accounts. Except for the reasons for the rapid progress of information technology in the SMC, we support a two-year transitional period, as it will be an even better and more cost-effective software and hardware for the debtors at that time. Significant progress is also being expected in the electronic literacy of users and in e-commerce solutions. In the next two years, global and regional trends are expected to accelerate the use of e-accounts among both micro and small enterprises and, last but not least, we can reasonably expect to improve accessibility to the global Internet, coverage with mobile frequencies and new generations, and to build the capacity of the Agency for Communications Networks and Services of the Republic of Slovenia to issue a certificate of possible inability to access the Internet, which is expected to be relatively rare after a transitional period. Given that more than half of the Member States of the European Union already know tax receipts, we do not need to look too far to find practical effects. Following the introduction of the tax rebates, which are, by comparability, still the closest version laid down in this proposal for a law, have recorded a number of positive effects, which we are also looking at in the SMC. In addition to the increase in the number of employees in the sectors where prior employees were either partially or fully paid in cash, and consequently taxes and contributions related to personal income, we are also counting on an increase in the volume of registered cash traffic and thus a greater inflow from value added tax, corporate income tax and activity tax. We also expect increased liquidity and capital strengthening of companies whose share of transport has bypassed the cashier's business and consequently the company's transaction accounts, which will also have a positive impact on improved payment disciplines and creditworthiness. As mentioned in our opinion, the proposal for a law on tax clearance of accounts is one of the key measures in the field of limiting the grey economy and additional tools for preventing employment and working in black. At the same time, it is worth stressing that this measure will achieve the objectives that we have set in relation to limiting the grey economy in the SMC only if a number of accompanying measures are also in place at the same time. In particular, we have here in mind measures in the field of customer awareness and the promotion of commitment to voluntary disclosure of traffic, which we believe would make a significant contribution to raising tax culture in Slovenia. The fact that this is significantly lower than the SMC would have wanted is significant that in 2013 the National Assembly had to even legislate the definition and the high fines for software that allowed automatic and systematic fraud by erasure and adaptation of cash registers, stocks and so on. We therefore do not miss the innovation of the Slovenians, but we would also like to redirect it to a place where it will be used for more constructive and socially responsible purposes. We also see tax clearance as an important step in this direction. Furthermore, in the SMC, we believe that, in order to be effective, the tax registers need to be highlighted in addition to the customer's awareness of their rights, as the fact that the proposed system does not work unless the cash account is issued. In the latter case, the customer may have a big word either as a silent partner to the seller in the absence of an invoice, to the extent that it is indifferent, or in certain cases even as an trigger, as we ourselves have been warned by the craftsmen that they are placed in an awkward position when customers sometimes even require that goods or services be provided without an account. In the foreign, such solutions already exist mainly in terms of the duty to keep the account for a shorter period. The bill seeks to address two cases: keeping an account when the purchase is made in the business premises of the debtor and when the service or goods are charged at home. In the SMC, we believe that the comments and press releases so far have developed into a solid debate, which is likely to be upgraded in a reasoned manner today, and then in the legislative process below, there are effective solutions, which in a suitable and broader manner would also address the customer's responsibilities and duties. At the same time, we are committed to further encouraging buyers with measures that have proven effective in the past and do not overburden the competent authority. We have in mind, of course, encouraging the commitment to demand an account and any self-initiative SMS or online verification of those when establishing a suitable reward fund. This solution is not part of this law, but we certainly consider it an important complementary measure that will help achieve the objectives set. Your Honor! In the SMC Group, we support the draft Law on tax clearance of accounts and thus the introduction of so-called online tax registers, because we believe that this is one of the key measures to limit the scope of the grey economy and an important additional tool to prevent employment and work in black. We believe that, even on the basis of today's reasoned debate in the National Assembly, the proposal can together be upgraded to the extent that it will receive broad support. Thank you.

If we already accept the idea that this State intervention is necessary to ensure public benefit in the form of reducing the grey economy and preventing tax evasion and fraud, we are certainly asking ourselves the urgency of this measure. In doing so, we may also find an answer to the question whether the state authorities may be responsible for the situation in the field of the grey economy with their own rigidity in exercising their powers. We are also further interested in the appropriateness of this measure in order to achieve the stated objective; in recent years, a lot of time and money has been devoted to measures to achieve the objective of reducing the grey economy pursued by the bill's proposer. Of course, this raises the question whether this is not yet one in a series of quick-to-do unconformed measures and, in particular, a measure without a prior appropriate analysis and without a proper evaluation of the recently implemented measures. In doing so, we may find that existing measures need only minor changes that would not affect as many entities as we will if the proposed law enters into force. It is not clear from the proposal for a law what percentage of the reduction in the grey economy we are talking about. They deal with a number of unnecessary red tape daily, often unable or difficult to obtain credit, although it is mostly very low-value loans. Smallness makes it difficult to achieve price competitiveness and presents a major challenge for them to buy new machines. The additional financial burden for most companies is a major problem. The problem is also the uneven development of Slovenia. For we still have areas where there is no or extremely poor coverage with internet connection. While this option has been foreseen by the proposer and, for these cases, the undertaking will have to prove the inability to establish an electronic connection by means of a certificate from the Agency for Communications Networks and Services. However, would it not be right to provide first Internet coverage across the entire territory of Slovenia? Such coverage would also have a positive impact on the development of less developed areas. According to the law, companies from already disadvantaged areas will have to obtain the Agency's certificates, so you are imposing additional administrative work on them. Nor can we support the fact that the customer is being transferred to the customer's side of the obligation to keep the account until the warranty period. Whether or not the buyer will keep an account for the exercise of the right to guarantee is and must remain its decision. If the customer wishes to protect himself in the event of the exercise of rights in the event of a defect or a defect in the goods, he is already motivated enough and will also save the account until the deadline is reached. We therefore find the proposed penalties unnecessary and disproportionate in terms of the level. In the Slovenian Democratic Party's Group we oppose additional burdens on the economy, we are advocates of the opposite – advocates of tax and administrative burdens. With the proposed measure, the introduction of the tax registers will not affect the grey economy, as the tax warehouses will not solve the problem of non-institution and will create an additional financial and administrative burden mainly for the small economy. Therefore, we will not support the proposed law on tax clearance of accounts. Thank you.
