ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u135	Thank you very much. Mrs Urska Ban has the floor to prepare the mag. Marco Pogačnik. Here you go, Mrs. Urska Ban.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u136	Thank you for your word, Mr. Vice President! We have already heard in the debate today that according to the Statistical Office of the Republic of Slovenia, the grey economy in Slovenia is for a good 8% or in money is 3 billion euros. The problem of the grey economy is practically detected in all sectoral areas. Today, as has already been said, we are dealing with only one pebble in the entire mosaic, i.e. activities related to cash payments, where it is found that the phenomenon of the grey economy is also the most present. This measure of the introduction of tax warehouses refers to the receipt of cash for the carriage of goods and services, it is said that it applies to all those providing goods and services for cash. This project, a project for the introduction of tax registers, is being introduced on the basis of concrete data and findings resulting from tax controls that have already been carried out so far. Indeed, in July 2014, by supplementing Article 38 of the Tax Procedure Act, so-called virtual tax warehouses have already been introduced. This article or this addition to the law specified what technical conditions the computer program must fulfil if someone wants to issue accounts using such a computer program and such a computer programme had to provide a audit trail. A number of inspections have also been carried out under this Decision and the conclusions of which can be drawn in two groups. That is to say that those taxpayers who have already used computer programs instead of upgrading or adapting these computer programs to provide an audit trail have rather decided to suspend such a method of issuing accounts and have moved back to issuing an account in paper form. Even those taxpayers who maintain computer-based accounts are still using programmes that allow undetected change, erasing of data in computer programs. On the basis of these findings, a new legal measure, which has been in force since February this year, is supplemented by Article 31 of the Tax Procedure Act, where, where the debtor decides to issue invoices in paper form, it is necessary to use the special bound accounts that are recorded in the Financial Administration and thus to ensure traceability in such a paper manner of invoices. However, since we confirmed this legal amendment, we have pointed out that more effective monitoring is needed through legal measures. This is reflected in a significant part of today's proposal for a law on tax clearance of accounts or on the so-called introduction of tax receipts, in the sense that they will be directly linked to the computer system of the Financial Administration, which will allow first control and first control to be carried out computerically and further controls can be carried out within the framework of the Financial Administration, where further consideration of the data to be received by the Financial Administration in connection with the invoices issued will continue. Following this proposal, the cashiers will be linked online to the Central Information System of the Financial Administration. The debtors will send invoices for which they receive cash payments before issue to the FURS, which will confirm and store account details in the real-time issuance process. The procedure for tax clearance of accounts, together with the existing and previously mentioned amendments to Articles 38 and 31 of the Tax Procedure Act, will create a system that will allow traceability and effectiveness of controls over invoices issued and further limit the grey economy. The fundamental idea of such systems is, as I have already said, that each cash account shall be sent before issue to the tax authority where it is validated and stored. The debtor may use any electronic device with the possibility of accessing the Internet to issue accounts. It is an open system of tax registers without certification of hardware or software. Except as has been pointed out today, ensuring access to the Internet does not generally require specific additional costs for the debtors. In this case, electronic devices are ordinary computers, tablet computers, smartphones and modern registers, all of course with the possibility of accessing the Internet, which in most cases is also the only condition for the operation of this system. On electronic devices, normal cash registering programmes are installed with additional functionality for the exchange of data with the tax authority. I believe that today, a large majority of businesses cannot operate if one of these forms of electronic device is not already available. It is likely that those who will have to do the whole system are very few, or those who do the work in black and do not issue the accounts today. In this way, a cost-effective and administratively less demanding system is introduced, with significantly better results. The tax authority will store data on all invoices issued in cash, allowing it to analyse the data, compare the data of the various types of taxes received and so on. On this basis, the tax authority will be able to carry out targeted controls and plan further measures. The system will allow the inspector to have real-time data available in the control procedures, which greatly enhances the effectiveness of control. For example, in real time, accounts will be displayed and monitored and invoices issued vary during the day, during a specified period of time for certain venues where the activity is carried out. The chosen solution for the introduction of the tax registers goes in this direction, which I have now said and is also cost-effective and administratively low or least burdensome for the debtors. We often hear in the public that the tax system is unfair because it allows taxpayers who should be treated differently in the same or comparable situation, because someone can avoid paying the tax and others cannot. It is therefore the task of the tax system to ensure a level playing field for all taxpayers through measures requiring a relatively low administrative burden on the taxpayers and in order to prevent tax evasion or abuse. As has already been said, the introduction of tax warehouses is one pebble in the mosaic of the many necessary measures necessary to increase the efficiency of collecting public benefits. The fact is that avoiding or not paying is large, probably not only because of the crisis or inability to pay, but also because of intentional non-payment of taxes. It is therefore necessary to find a balanced combination of measures that will relate to increasing the efficiency of the collection of taxes, and I am talking about the development of tax culture here. Unfortunately, we have come so far in Slovenia that the concept of tax culture is almost profanity. We simply do not want or develop tax cultures, so we are always arguing about individual segments and not about a whole society that can effectively collect taxes. And until we win this tax culture or accept it, by then we'll probably be talking about whether to take a coffee bill, a savings guarantee, the amount of the costs to set up the tax registers and so on. In summary, the introduction of tax boxes is certainly an important element in improving the efficiency and fairness of the tax system in Slovenia. This is a measure to upgrade the efforts so far to reduce the number of infringements in this area. Of course, this cannot be the only measure, so it must be taken together with other accompanying activities or complementary measures, which have already been discussed today. It is necessary to find a way to increase the tax culture of customers and to increase the customer's awareness of how important it is to request and also take the account. The main purpose of the introduction of tax registers is therefore, in addition to the effective collection of taxes, of the awareness that we are developing, and this will also enable us a better country. If we all contribute to this and start talking about taxes in a different way, then we will probably eventually get even shorter waiting lines, better healthcare, higher pensions, better school system and so on. I think that our common goal is for the state to establish a tax collection system that will cover the widest possible range of people who are in fact also taxpayers to reach these goals that I have said. And this bill is certainly a step in this direction. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u139	Thank you very much. She asked the Mag for a word. Matthew Vraničar, State Secretary at the Ministry of Finance. Here you go.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u140	Thank you very much. Some specific questions have been asked and I would like to answer them. First of all, forestry – yes, forestry is one of the emerging forms of the grey economy. In my previous intervention, I mentioned that we are not pretending to be able to address all the emerging forms of the grey economy with tax registers, but in an indirect sense we can also address the non-issue of accounts. Today, the Financial Administration, with the exception of field control, has no possibility of identifying the non-issue of accounts. By means of a tax system, by matching data on the deduction of entry tax due to the purchase of goods and services for the provision of taxable activities and, on the other hand, by not expressing revenue or exit tax and not issuing invoices, it will be possible to detect those who, on the one hand, enforce the deduction of the entry tax and, on the other hand, do not issue the invoices and thus do not pay that exit tax to the added value that they should pay under law. However, as I mentioned earlier, the tax warehouses cannot and are not designed to address all the emerging forms of the grey economy. They enable, as I said, one of the instruments of improved control and enable the staff capacity of the Financial Administration to effectively focus on those emerging forms of the grey economy and other forms of tax evasion that cannot be covered by tax registers. Other areas, tax debt, flat-rate taxation - I agree, these are areas to be addressed, and the Government has adopted a comprehensive plan of projects in the tax area to be undertaken or already implemented in 2015 and the very area of tax debt and to examine the effectiveness of existing flat-rate taxation are two of the projects that we are addressing in this context. I would like to mention that, not only because of the improved economic situation, but also because of the effective part of the Financial Administration, the tax debt has been reduced by 250 million in the last year and a half. It is still at a high level, unacceptablely high, and additional measures need to be taken to reduce this debt. In particular, the so-called active debt, which has been declining rapidly over the last period, but not in the way of essentially moving into an inactive or bookable debt, is called an irrevocable tax debt, but by actually reducing the total amount of tax debt. Flat-rate taxation – since late April we have received first data on the actual implementation of the current flat-rate taxation system. We need some time to analyse this data to find out what the shortcomings of the existing system are. And we will certainly meet frequently in the coming months at such meetings of the National Assembly because we plan to bring a number of proposals to the National Assembly this year, amendments to the various tax rules that will address tax burden restructuring on the one hand, on the other hand removing administrative barriers and on the third hand increasing efficiency in collecting public taxes. It is not possible to submit all the laws at once, so the Government has drawn up a plan on how to prepare individual measures and forward them to the National Assembly with the aim of making the main things, at least the most important ones, adopted by the end of this year. Banks and mail – there is no exception to the bill. Therefore, both for banking services and for postal services or goods supplied by them, which are sold via mail, account validation will have to be ensured. During the transitional period, these debtors, like the others, have the option to decide whether to immediately switch to the electronic validation of accounts or to use, in particular, the bound account book for field workers, postmen working on the ground, and subsequently enter it into the validation system, where this is likely to be the case. As far as online commerce and this PayPal payment system are concerned; in fact, the bill refers to cash payments, direct payments to the supplier or service provider. To the extent that payment is made through a payment service provider, which PayPal is essentially, then, by the very provision of the law itself, does not fall within this framework, but I agree that during the period until the second hearing, it is necessary to check whether the provisions relating to this matter are sufficiently clear and with the corresponding additions to the law in this direction to be amended or improved. Last but not least, this is the very purpose of such debates as today. The cost for an individual debtor; I have already mentioned that it depends on what system the debtors are using at the moment, and these costs range from EUR 0 to EUR 300 after our assessment, but of course we did not take into account the minimum required equipment to have in order to confirm the account. We have not taken into account the fact that the debtors want to link this system to keeping stock records, to their books of invoices issued and so on. The costs for the Financial Administration; the total costs, according to our estimate, are up to a maximum of two million euros and relate to the development of computer equipment that will enable the validation of accounts, as well as the development of tools or tools that will enable the preparation of the analysis, the carrying out of a risk analysis of a terrible large number of data to be obtained by the Financial Administration for this purpose. Selection of suppliers; this is precisely the advantage of the system that we propose: it does not require any selection of suppliers for computerised cashiers in taxpayers. The only thing we demand from taxpayers is to ensure connectivity with the computer system of the Financial Administration. And there can be no lobbying in the sense that only individual tax box solutions can be taken into account, because we consciously have not decided on a system that they know, say, in Serbia or what data we are looking for in Austria, to certify a computer, computer equipment, a printer with which a taxly recognised account is actually issued. As regards the selection of suppliers to supplement or upgrade the computer system of the Financial Administration, these procedures are carried out in accordance with the Public Procurement Act. Thank you very much.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u141	Thank you very much. Marija Antonija Kovacič has the floor to prepare Mr Matjaž Germany.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u142	Thank you, Mr. Vice President, for your word. Greetings to Madam Secretary and to the team, honourable Members and Members of Parliament! As I have already mentioned in my position, this is a completely new law, which has been repeatedly discussed by including comments also from a broader public debate. This is the validation of accounts which constitutes an upgrade of the current system for the issuance of invoices using software which is in accordance with the procedure for the validation of accounts in cash operations by the State authority at the time of the issuance of the invoice. In doing so, we want to prevent the problem of the grey economy. Tax receipts will be compulsory for all taxpayers operating in cash, based on two bases, namely the online system of tax receipts following a prescribed procedure for verifying the accounts of the tax authority at the time of issuing the invoices and the use of the bound account book only in exceptional cases where there is no Internet, or in certain situations where an unforeseen electricity problem occurs. Due to these exceptional situations, the final provisions laid down in the law provide for a two-year extension of the period of entry into force of the law. The law also provides news about the co-responsibility of preventing the grey economy to customers of business services. We must demand an account and also keep it in evidence of possible control. This also involves the buyer in the control to ensure a fair distribution of the tax burden. The fines for an unissued account or for the non-absorbing of an account will be inconsequential, which has also triggered much controversy in the public. In the Coalition Treaty, we have committed ourselves to combating the grey economy and establishing payment discipline. The draft law before us is part of the whole process for the enforcement of tax boxes, which have proved to be an effective measure in most countries to combat the grey economy. To prevent the subsequent processing of data on taxation and avoidance of taxpayers' payments in cash operations, to ensure a reliable recording of the cash traffic of taxpayers, to ensure more efficient collection of compulsory duties, to establish more equitable conditions of business, to regulate the market, to prevent unfair competition and to speed up economic development, to protect the consumer and to prevent fraud are the objectives for which we will take part in our parliamentary group, so I will also support the bill's proposal to confirm the accounts. Thank you.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u176	Thank you for your word, Mr. President. Dear representatives of the government, colleagues and colleagues! Today, we have heard several figures on the problem, which points to the problem of the grey economy, but it may not be too much to point out that according to the official data of the Statistical Office of the Republic of Slovenia for 2010 all adjustments to gross social product amount to almost 3 and half billion euros and 10% of GDP. Now within this scope, the concept of a grey economy is particularly interesting in this law, which is used almost exclusively in terms of tax evasion, which amounts to 1.3 billion euros and 3.6% of GDP. Furthermore, within this area of tax evasion, SURS notes that in addition to black work, in addition to the part of the units that are not registered at all, the most problematic job-free work is, according to SURS, especially in small businesses with up to three employees. Today we have already heard a lot about these poor small businesses and entrepreneurs who are supposed to be most affected by this law, but the SURS data also mention that there are also small businesses among tax collectors. Of course, the law does not directly address the problem of not issuing invoices, but, as State Secretary Vraničar has already said, this law will release the human resources capacity to prosecute tax evasion in this spectrum as well. Why are we even talking about the gray economy? Not so much because of the color, but because it has many negative consequences: higher tax burden on taxpayers who respect the legal order that they pay; unfair access by individuals to public services – that is to say those who do not pay taxes – represents unfair competition on the market, the inability to enforce consumer complaints, and the general lack of confidence in the law that we are trying to improve – not all Members. It is therefore necessary to prevent the grey economy and, in addition to existing measures, require new and more effective measures to limit the grey economy. What were those measures already in place? On the basis of the findings of the Financial Administration of the Republic of Slovenia before 2013, that some taxpayers operating in cash use computer programs and hardware that allow for the deletion of all invoices issued, the deletion of individual items in invoices and the miscalculation of all traffic, and the cash at the level of such deleted traffic was then mostly used for cash payments of workers, both those who are said to have been – in addition – employed by law and those who were employed in black and for personal use. Therefore, Article 38 of the Tax Procedure Act, which enacted the Virtual Tax Procedure, adopted further measures in 2013, which followed and entered into force in January 2015, namely this year, and Article 31a of the Tax Procedure Act, which provides that taxpayers must apply the invoices from the FURS, which are subject to prior approval by FURS, in respect of taxes on cash transactions without the application of the relevant computer program or electronic device. However, the reason for introducing this solution was greater traceability in the issuance of cash accounts without the use of electronic devices and the provision of appropriate operating conditions for debtors using different means of issuing invoices. Why I stress this change in particular – because in the present law during the transitional period it also allows for the choice that those who have used this method should retain it by 2017. What did the financial inspection find so far? During the period 2009-2014, it carried out more than 16 thousand controls, in which tax evasions amounted to EUR 11.4 million, of which more than EUR 3.5 million were detected in 2014. The measures taken, the previous ones, therefore, have positive effects, but inspection controls statistics show that the current model of virtual tax warehouses, as we have already heard today, is not completed or is insufficient because the information on invoices issued is kept in the register, by the debtor and not by the tax authority and is not required to provide specific technical protection for these data, so that they can still be modified without an audit trail. The reasons for the introduction of tax warehouses, those proposed by the law, are therefore justified: for the first time, on the basis of the SURS assessments made with internationally comparable methodologies and on the basis of past tax controls. With regard to the technical solution proposed by the law, i.e. online tax remittances, these are those in which the exchange of information on invoices issued with the tax authority takes place in real time is more than obvious, in my opinion, compared to classical tax warehouses and also with the system we have so far. In fact, in order to send invoices to the tax authority in real time, the possibility of retrofitting transport data without an audit trail is reduced to a minimum. The system is open, meaning that the debtor can use any electronic device, i.e. a personal laptop, a tablet computer, a smartphone, an electronic register box, so in the event that the debtor already has such a device, there is no additional cost for the purchase of special equipment, which would be between €300 and €800 for a special so-called fiscal printer, certified software and hardware by the tax authority, in order to ensure greater competition in the marketing of retail solutions and consequently a lower price for end-users. Since the information on invoices issued to the tax authority is available, the inspectors may also exercise tax controls without having to visit the debtor, thereby establishing a more effective supervisory mechanism for higher risk debtors. Here I return to the introductions of the above mentioned small entrepreneurs with a maximum of three employees, for whom the SURS notes that they are among the most represented among tax offenders and that targeted controls can be carried out, which also means the release of staff resources from tax controls. The modified method of control will also entail a lower burden on the debtors as the number of direct visits by the tax authority to the taxpayers with a view to exercising control will be reduced. As far as the costs are concerned, some assessments have been quite exaggerated here today. The software solutions to be used to exchange data with FURS are not so difficult and, in accordance with the existing tax rules, it will be possible to recognise the costs of purchasing tax warehouses and internet access arrangements as tax-recognised expenditure which reduces the tax base, as State Secretary Vraničarova has already said. In the case of the purchase of computer equipment, the taxable person may apply an depreciation rate of 50 % in the year of purchase, and in addition, the taxable persons may, in accordance with the legislation already in force, impose a tax relief for the investment in the tax box — some, i.e. under Article 55a of the Law on the Income of Legal Persons, where they may impose a reduction in the tax base of 40% of the investment amount after receipt and the intangible assets, but most of all in the amount of the tax base; others, on the basis of Article 66a of the Income Act, where they may impose a reduction in the tax base of 40 % of the invested amount of the equipment and the intangible long-term assets in the tax year of investment. Finally, the introduction of online tax registers should also be assessed in the context of the possibility of developing technology in the field of trade, such as the development of smart phones applications or dedicated computer programs for business management, so that entrepreneurs can be more user-friendly and thus more competitive. Thank you very much.

ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u183	Mrs Marusha Škopac has the floor to prepare Mr Jan Mahnic and Mrs Marija Bacič.
ParlaMint-SI_2015-05-19-SDZ7-Redna-08.ana.u184	Thank you for your word, Mr. President! Today we are talking about a proposal for a law that will prescribe mandatory tax receipts that will have to be used in the case of cash transactions. Companies that don't do business in cash, the real thing is that all their payments are made in cash, they're not going to need tax receipts. There are two types of tax receipts in the world. This is how we know classic tax receipts based on specific technologies for the safe storage of data locally in the treasury. Specific technical requirements are laid down for the protection of data and for the recording of transactions in the register. Such tax receipts are actually quite widespread, as they are now used by 15 countries in Europe, namely Italy, Greece, Poland, Latvia, Lithuania, Sweden, Belgium, Bulgaria, Hungary, Serbia, Malta, Russia and Turkey. In addition, such cashiers are also used in Argentina and Brazil. In Slovenia, however, we opted for an online tax system where the exchange of information on invoices issued with the tax authority takes place in real time. Such a system has already been successfully implemented before us by Croatia. The advantage of this system is that the exchange of goods, except in rare cases, will not be necessary. The debtor needs internet access to link goods with the tax authority's information system and the data transfer programme. It is therefore an open tax box system without certified hardware or software. In addition, in neighbouring Austria, there is a large-scale tax reform in progress, which will introduce tax rebates, as we did with 1. 1. 2016 and 2017 is also expected to introduce tax rebates in Germany. From this I can conclude that all countries are increasingly aware of the problems and scope of the grey economy and are looking for and introducing solutions to reduce it. So Slovenia will be nothing special. Let us remember the shouting and shouting in Croatia before the introduction of the tax cans of the inn and all the other shareholders of the mostly cash business. Today, it is clear that all their threats were totally unfounded. From the data available to us, it is estimated that traffic in Croatian guest houses increased by 68% in 2013, the number of employees increased by 32%, the average net salary by 8%. In the course of the night, the losses of Croatian innkeepers turned into profits and, instead of reducing the number of innkeepers, their number increased by 18% in the first year of the introduction of the tax registers. In neighbouring Croatia, therefore, the law has achieved more than its purpose. One of the objectives of the introduction of tax warehouses is to create more equitable conditions for business, labour market regulation, to prevent unfair competition and to accelerate economic development. And if we return to the results of Croatia in the introduction of the tax registers, these objectives have undoubtedly been achieved. The aim of the law is also to ensure a more efficient collection of compulsory duties, which will also mean a fairer distribution of the existing tax burden. It will be possible to identify the risk of taxpayers already on the basis of directly received data from tax repositories and, accordingly, to strengthen control in those undertakings where the anomaly will be detected. This means that the staff resources of the tax authority will be much more effectively targeted. Members of Parliament have also received comments from the Slovenian Consumers' Union on the bill on the validation of accounts. Their comments relate mainly to paragraphs 12 and 17 of the Act, which refers to a fine for customers in the event that it does not take over the account. The Union of Consumers of Slovenia strongly opposes the argument that the consumer cannot bear the burden of control over the correctness of the issuance of invoices and the removal of taxes. Let me stress that this does not control the correctness of the issue of the account and does not take over the work of the supervisory authorities. The customer is only obliged to request and take over the account, because, if he does not do so, the non-issuer of the account has actually been able to avoid paying the tax and from this point of view allowed the seller to tax evasion, and this is the important part of the tax culture which is required to be thoroughly upgraded in our society. However, the comments of the Slovenian Consumer Association regarding the maintenance of the account until the period of guarantee are examined. If we now look in detail at the third paragraph of Article 12 of the Law, it is said that the customer must take over and keep the account until the period of the guarantee period when the service is provided in the customer's premises. Since in this case the service is performed at the customer's premises, where we know that there are currently the biggest tax avoidances, the customer's awareness that he has to demand and take over the account is even greater. As beautifully described in the government's draft law, each tax box model is just one piece in the mosaic of measures against the grey economy and tax evasion. In order to achieve the effectiveness of such a system, effective control and awareness of the importance of voluntary compliance with tax obligations is essential.
